# Product Requirements Blueprint (PRD)

## Personal Portfolio & ATS CV Generator

![Minimalist Portfolio Hero Section – Clean, Focused, and Engaging by Chamod Dilshan on Dribbble](https://images.openai.com/static-rsc-4/8xt0AOCZ_heKGBSWuuoJpCMjTTgZViafthQnNLeNhyLlUekKpEJl1FGpsQmF1sfZaJdRFOi3yMYK30C9LVukCbTc-_BouZTsZtxOpWqvV3YfFHEVT9B7QM6k8vWusBUTT2MmK1ubh_oeiimzjFWWliTCkfWeNsybA0HBQ4zun4o?purpose=inline)

MVP defined

Personal use

Web-based

Online deployment

Document type: Product Requirements Document · Version 1.0 · 4 October 2026

## 1. Product overview

### 1.1 Product vision

Build a personal career management website that allows one administrator to maintain professional information in a private dashboard, publish selected information through a public portfolio, and generate multiple ATS-friendly CVs in PDF format from the same centralized data.

The design direction is hybrid minimalist: a clean, professional foundation with creative project showcases and subtle personal branding. The CV output prioritizes readability and conventional document structure over decorative design.

### 1.2 Problem statement

Managing a professional portfolio and multiple CVs separately creates repetitive work, inconsistent information, and difficulty adapting a CV to different job opportunities.

The product addresses this by separating career data from its presentation. Information is entered once and can be reused in the public portfolio and any number of saved CV configurations.

### 1.3 Product objectives

1. Centralize professional information in one private management interface.

2. Allow career records to be created, edited, deleted and reused without editing website code.

3. Publish selected information to an online portfolio accessible to recruiters.

4. Generate and download multiple role-specific CVs as PDFs.

5. Support automatic content suggestions alongside manual content selection.

6. Maintain clear control over which information is public and which is private.

### 1.4 Scope and boundaries

| In scope for MVP                   | Out of scope for MVP            |
| ---------------------------------- | ------------------------------- |
| One administrator account          | Public user registration        |
| Profile and career data management | Multi-user collaboration        |
| Public portfolio pages             | Paid plans or subscriptions     |
| Multiple saved CV configurations   | AI-generated CV content         |
| Manual CV content selection        | Automatic AI job matching       |
| Rule-based content suggestions     | Drag-and-drop document editor   |
| CV templates and PDF export        | Job application tracking        |
| Public/private content controls    | Recruiter accounts or messaging |

Product principle: this is a content management system for your own professional identity, not a recruitment platform or a general-purpose CV design editor.

## 2. User roles and permissions

The MVP has two user types in terms of access, but only one authenticated account.

Administrator — you

Authenticated access to the private dashboard.

* Manage profile and all career records.

* Create and manage CV configurations.

* Publish or unpublish portfolio content.

* Preview, generate and download CV PDFs.

* Manage account and site settings.

Public visitor — recruiter or employer

No login required.

* View published profile and portfolio content.

* Browse published projects and experience.

* Open permitted external links.

* Download a CV only if the owner explicitly publishes or links to it.

* Cannot edit, create, or view private records.

Private dashboard routes and private career data must remain inaccessible to visitors even if they know a record's URL.

## 3. Information architecture and page structure

The application is divided into three experiences: the private dashboard, the public portfolio and the CV generation workflow.

### A. Private dashboard

Authenticated pages for managing information.

Dashboard overview

Summary cards, recent updates, portfolio status, and quick actions.

Profile

Personal information, professional summary, contact details and social links.

Career content

Projects, experiences, education, skills, certifications and achievements.

Portfolio manager

Portfolio preview, visibility controls, featured content and public site settings.

CV manager

Saved CV versions, role selection, content selection, templates, preview and export.

Settings

Account security, public contact preferences and data backup.

### B. Public portfolio

Home

Name, headline, short introduction, featured projects and primary links.

About

Professional background, education, selected skills and relevant experience.

Projects & experience

Project gallery, individual project detail pages and selected career history.

Contact

Published contact details and external professional profiles.

### C. CV workflow

Create or duplicate a CV

Set a name and target role.

Select and order content

Apply suggested entries, manually adjust selection and arrange sections.

Choose template

Select a supported ATS-oriented design.

Preview and export

Review the result, resolve validation warnings and download the PDF.


## 4. Detailed MVP functional requirements

The following requirements define what the first release must do. IDs are included so each requirement can later be traced to a development task and a test case.

### 4.1 Authentication and dashboard

| ID      | Requirement                                                                                   | Priority |
| ------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- |
| AUTH-01 | The administrator can log in using valid credentials.                                         | Must     |
| AUTH-02 | Invalid credentials are rejected with a clear, non-revealing error.                           | Must     |
| AUTH-03 | Private dashboard pages require an authenticated session.                                     | Must     |
| AUTH-04 | The administrator can log out and invalidate the session.                                     | Must     |
| DASH-01 | The dashboard displays counts of projects, experiences, education records and certifications. | Must     |
| DASH-02 | Quick actions are available to add content and create a CV.                                   | Should   |
| DASH-03 | The dashboard indicates whether the public portfolio is published.                            | Should   |

### 4.2 Profile management

| ID      | Requirement                                                                        | Priority |
| ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- |
| PROF-01 | The administrator can create and edit the main professional profile.               | Must     |
| PROF-02 | The administrator can add and edit contact and professional links.                 | Must     |
| PROF-03 | Optional fields may be left blank without blocking the profile from being saved.   | Must     |
| PROF-04 | The administrator can preview the public presentation of the profile.              | Must     |
| PROF-05 | The administrator can control the visibility of public-facing contact information. | Must     |

### 4.3 Career content management

| ID      | Requirement                                                                                              | Priority |
| ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- |
| DATA-01 | The administrator can create, view, edit and delete career records.                                      | Must     |
| DATA-02 | Records support start dates, end dates and a current/ongoing status where relevant.                      | Must     |
| DATA-03 | Projects can store technologies, responsibilities, outcomes and external links.                          | Must     |
| DATA-04 | Records can be marked as public or private independently of CV selection.                                | Must     |
| DATA-05 | Records can be marked as featured for the portfolio.                                                     | Should   |
| DATA-06 | Records can be reordered where display order matters.                                                    | Should   |
| DATA-07 | Deleting a record that is used in saved CVs triggers a warning and safe handling of affected selections. | Must     |

### 4.4 Public portfolio

| ID      | Requirement                                                                            | Priority |
| ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- |
| PORT-01 | Public visitors can access the portfolio without logging in.                           | Must     |
| PORT-02 | The portfolio displays the latest saved version of published profile information.      | Must     |
| PORT-03 | Only published career records appear on public pages.                                  | Must     |
| PORT-04 | The administrator can feature and unfeature projects.                                  | Must     |
| PORT-05 | Project detail pages show the project's available description, technologies and links. | Must     |
| PORT-06 | Public pages are usable on desktop and mobile screen sizes.                            | Must     |
| PORT-07 | A CV download link appears only when an explicitly published CV is available.          | Should   |

### 4.5 CV manager and PDF generation

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                                             | Priority |
| ----- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- |
| CV-01 | The administrator can create multiple named CV configurations.                                                          | Must     |
| CV-02 | Each CV can specify a target role, professional summary and template.                                                   | Must     |
| CV-03 | The administrator can manually select individual projects, experiences and other entries.                               | Must     |
| CV-04 | The system can suggest relevant entries based on target role and record metadata.                                       | Must     |
| CV-05 | The administrator can accept or reject each suggested entry.                                                            | Must     |
| CV-06 | The administrator can reorder sections and selected entries within supported layouts.                                   | Must     |
| CV-07 | The administrator can duplicate, rename and delete a CV configuration.                                                  | Must     |
| CV-08 | The administrator can preview the generated CV before exporting it.                                                     | Must     |
| CV-09 | The system generates a downloadable PDF containing selectable text.                                                     | Must     |
| CV-10 | The CV uses conventional headings and a logical reading order.                                                          | Must     |
| CV-11 | The system warns when required information is missing or content may overflow the chosen template.                      | Must     |
| CV-12 | Changes to source records are reflected in future exports, while saved content selections remain intact where possible. | Must     |
| CV-13 | The system validates the exported PDF's extracted text and basic document completeness during testing.                  | Must     |

Clarification on automatic suggestions: for the MVP, these should be deterministic and explainable rather than AI-based. For example, if a CV targets Data Analyst, the system can prioritize records tagged with data analytics, SQL, Python, BI or related skills. The administrator remains responsible for the final selection.

### 4.6 Templates

| ID      | Requirement                                                                                     | Priority |
| ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- |
| TEMP-01 | The MVP offers at least two distinct CV templates.                                              | Must     |
| TEMP-02 | Templates prioritize ATS-oriented reading order, conventional headings and readable typography. | Must     |
| TEMP-03 | The administrator can preview a template before choosing it.                                    | Must     |
| TEMP-04 | Template changes do not alter the underlying career data or saved content selections.           | Must     |
| TEMP-05 | The system warns if content cannot fit cleanly in a selected layout.                            | Must     |

The two initial templates should be:

* Classic: single-column, text-first, conventional professional CV.

* Modern Minimal: single-column with subtle accent styling and restrained visual hierarchy.

Neither template should rely on icons, decorative graphics or visual columns to communicate essential qualifications.

## 5. Detailed form fields

These are the proposed form specifications for the first release. Fields marked optional should not block saving when empty. Required fields should be validated before submission.

### 5.1 Professional profile

| Field                 | Required? | Purpose                                       |
| --------------------- | --------- | --------------------------------------------- |
| Full name             | Yes       | Primary identity                              |
| Professional headline | Yes       | Short role or career description              |
| Professional summary  | No        | Introduction for portfolio and default CV     |
| Profile photo         | No        | Portfolio visual                              |
| Email                 | No        | Professional contact                          |
| Phone                 | No        | Contact information                           |
| City / country        | No        | General location                              |
| LinkedIn URL          | No        | Professional profile                          |
| GitHub URL            | No        | Technical work                                |
| Other links           | No        | Personal website, blog or additional profiles |
| Portfolio slug        | Yes       | Public URL identifier                         |

The full name and headline should be required to create a complete public profile. Contact details remain optional, since a person may prefer to direct recruiters to LinkedIn rather than publish a phone number.

### 5.2 Projects

| Field                   | Required?          | Purpose                                             |
| ----------------------- | ------------------ | --------------------------------------------------- |
| Project title           | Yes                | Identification                                      |
| Short summary           | Yes                | Card and CV description                             |
| Detailed description    | No                 | Portfolio project detail                            |
| Role / contribution     | No                 | Individual responsibilities                         |
| Project type            | No                 | Academic, personal, freelance, organizational, etc. |
| Start date / end date   | No                 | Timeline                                            |
| Currently ongoing       | No                 | Active project status                               |
| Technologies / tools    | No                 | Skills and technical context                        |
| Key responsibilities    | No                 | Work performed                                      |
| Outcomes / achievements | No                 | Results and impact                                  |
| Repository URL          | No                 | Source code                                         |
| Demo URL                | No                 | Live application or presentation                    |
| Cover image             | No                 | Portfolio showcase                                  |
| Featured                | Yes, default false | Homepage placement                                  |
| Published               | Yes, default false | Public visibility                                   |

A project may be saved before all its details are complete. The project title and short summary are required so that the record can be meaningfully identified and selected for the portfolio or CV.

### 5.3 Experience and organizational involvement

Use one reusable experience form for employment, internships, freelance work, organizational roles, volunteering and leadership positions.

| Field                   | Required?          | Purpose                                                       |
| ----------------------- | ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Organization name       | Yes                | Company, institution or organization                          |
| Position / role         | Yes                | Role title                                                    |
| Experience category     | Yes                | Employment, internship, organization, volunteering, freelance |
| Location                | No                 | General location                                              |
| Start date              | No                 | Timeline                                                      |
| End date                | No                 | Timeline                                                      |
| Currently active        | Yes, default false | Ongoing role status                                           |
| Description             | No                 | Overview of responsibilities                                  |
| Responsibilities        | No                 | Tasks performed                                               |
| Achievements / outcomes | No                 | Impact and results                                            |
| Related skills          | No                 | Associated skills                                             |
| Published               | Yes, default false | Public visibility                                             |

A single experience form avoids creating separate management systems for internships, campus organizations, volunteering and employment.

### 5.4 Education

| Field                          | Required?          | Purpose                            |
| ------------------------------ | ------------------ | ---------------------------------- |
| Institution name               | Yes                | University, school or institution  |
| Degree / qualification         | No                 | Academic program                   |
| Field of study                 | No                 | Major or specialization            |
| Start date                     | No                 | Timeline                           |
| End date / expected graduation | No                 | Timeline                           |
| Current student status         | Yes, default false | Indicates ongoing study            |
| GPA / grade                    | No                 | Optional academic information      |
| Description / activities       | No                 | Relevant coursework or involvement |
| Published                      | Yes, default false | Public visibility                  |

### 5.5 Skills

| Field                          | Required?          | Purpose                                                        |
| ------------------------------ | ------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Skill name                     | Yes                | e.g. SQL, Python, communication                                |
| Skill category                 | Yes                | Technical, analytical, business, interpersonal, language, etc. |
| Proficiency level              | No                 | Optional self-assessed level                                   |
| Related projects / experiences | No                 | Supporting evidence                                            |
| Published                      | Yes, default false | Public visibility                                              |

Proficiency levels should be optional. The application should not present self-assessed skill levels as objectively verified.

### 5.6 Certifications and achievements

| Field                | Required?          | Purpose                         |
| -------------------- | ------------------ | ------------------------------- |
| Title                | Yes                | Certificate or achievement name |
| Issuing organization | No                 | Issuer or awarding body         |
| Issue date           | No                 | Date awarded                    |
| Expiration date      | No                 | Validity, if applicable         |
| Credential ID        | No                 | Verification identifier         |
| Credential URL       | No                 | Verification link               |
| Description          | No                 | Context or achievement details  |
| Published            | Yes, default false | Public visibility               |

### 5.7 CV configuration

| Field                               | Required?          | Purpose                             |
| ----------------------------------- | ------------------ | ----------------------------------- |
| CV name                             | Yes                | e.g. Data Analyst CV                |
| Target role                         | Yes                | Intended job category               |
| Professional summary override       | No                 | Role-specific introduction          |
| Selected template                   | Yes                | PDF layout                          |
| Included sections                   | Yes                | Sections to render                  |
| Selected career records             | Yes                | Entries included in the CV          |
| Section order                       | Yes                | Document reading order              |
| Entry order                         | Yes                | Ordering within sections            |
| Suggested entries accepted/rejected | No                 | Manual overrides of recommendations |
| Published CV status                 | Yes, default false | Controls public availability        |
| Last generated date                 | System-generated   | Export tracking                     |

For the first release, a CV configuration should reference career records instead of duplicating them. Its selection list and ordering are saved independently, allowing the same project or experience to appear in several CVs.

### 5.8 Settings

* Administrator credentials and password management.

* Portfolio publication status.

* Public contact visibility.

* Public portfolio slug.

* Optional default CV download.

* Data export and backup actions.

Authentication credentials must never be included in public profile data or exported CV content.

Expected behavior

* An incomplete profile can be saved as a draft.

* Unpublished records remain accessible in the private dashboard.

* The portfolio can be previewed before public release.

* Publishing the portfolio must not automatically publish every career record.

Expected behavior

* Form validation identifies missing required fields.

* Successful saving persists the project.

* Editing a published project updates its public presentation.

* Editing a private project never makes it public automatically.

* Existing CV configurations retain their selection of the project if its record is edited.

Expected behavior

* Suggestions are optional; the administrator can manually select content.

* The administrator can reject an individual suggestion without losing the source record.

* A CV can be saved as a draft before export.

* Export validation checks for missing required profile data and obvious layout problems.

* A successful export produces a PDF containing selectable text and the expected sections.

* Generating a PDF does not publish the CV to the public portfolio.

Expected behavior

A source-data update should not force the administrator to recreate CV configurations. However, an already downloaded PDF is a static file and will not change automatically. The administrator must generate a new PDF to reflect the latest information.

If a selected record is deleted, the system must warn the administrator and handle the affected CV references safely. It must not silently substitute a different record.

## 7. Data entities and relationships

The conceptual data model below defines the main entities, their responsibilities and how they relate. This is a logical model, not a database implementation specification.

Administrator & Profile

Account access and the main professional identity

Career records

Projects · Experiences · Education · Skills · Certifications · Achievements

Referenced by

CV configurations

Target role · Template · Selected entries · Section order · Overrides

Generated CV exports

PDF files representing a particular CV configuration at export time

### 7.1 Entity definitions

| Entity                   | Main attributes                                                     | Purpose                                             |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| Administrator            | Account ID, login identifier, authentication data                   | Restricts private access to the owner               |
| Profile                  | Name, headline, summary, photo, contact and social links            | Stores the main professional identity               |
| Project                  | Title, descriptions, dates, technologies, links, image, visibility  | Stores portfolio and project CV content             |
| Experience               | Organization, role, category, dates, responsibilities, achievements | Stores employment and organizational history        |
| Education                | Institution, qualification, field, dates, GPA, description          | Stores academic background                          |
| Skill                    | Name, category, optional proficiency                                | Maintains a reusable skill list                     |
| Certification            | Title, issuer, dates, credential information                        | Stores certifications and awards                    |
| CV configuration         | Name, target role, summary override, template, visibility           | Represents a saved CV version                       |
| CV section configuration | Section identifier, enabled status, order                           | Defines the structure of a CV                       |
| CV record selection      | CV ID, record type, record ID, order, selection source              | Connects selected career entries to a particular CV |
| CV export                | CV ID, generation time, file reference, status                      | Tracks exported PDF versions                        |
| Portfolio settings       | Slug, publication status, contact visibility, featured items        | Controls the public website                         |

### 7.2 Relationship rules

* One administrator owns one main profile.

* One profile can have many projects, experiences, education records, skills and certifications.

* One CV configuration can reference many career records.

* One career record can be referenced by many CV configurations.

* One CV configuration can have multiple export snapshots over time.

* Public visibility and CV selection are independent properties.

* A project marked private may still be included in a private CV export.

* A published portfolio does not imply that all generated CVs are public.

Skills can be associated with several projects and experiences. The relationship between skills and career records should support multiple associations rather than storing all skill names as unstructured text.

### 7.3 Data lifecycle rules

1. Saving a form updates the canonical career record.

2. A CV configuration stores selections and ordering, not a duplicate of every career record.

3. Preview and export use the latest saved source data.

4. Exported PDFs remain snapshots of their generation time.

5. Deleting a career record requires checking its references in saved CV configurations.

6. Publishing a record changes public visibility but does not change its inclusion in any CV.

7. Unpublishing a record removes it from public pages without deleting its underlying data.

These rules are essential to keep the portfolio, private dashboard and CV exports consistent.


## 8. Acceptance criteria

Acceptance criteria define the observable conditions that must be met before the MVP can be considered complete. They should be testable without relying on assumptions about how the application is built.

### 8.1 Functional acceptance tests

### Authentication and privacy

0 of 16 acceptance tests checked

Reset

An authorized administrator can log in and reach the dashboard.

Invalid credentials do not grant access.

Unauthenticated visitors cannot access private dashboard pages or data.

Logging out prevents access to protected pages through the previous session.

A private project never appears on public portfolio pages.

Public profile pages expose only information designated for publication.

A public visitor cannot access a CV configuration or private PDF merely by guessing its URL.

Required fields show clear validation errors and preserve entered values when validation fails.

A project can be created, edited, viewed and deleted.

Editing a published project updates its public presentation without creating a duplicate.

The same project can be included in one CV and excluded from another.

A CV configuration retains its selections and ordering after being saved and reopened.

Changing a CV template does not alter the source career records.

The generated PDF contains the expected selected sections and entries in the intended order.

The PDF has selectable text, readable headings and no unintended clipping or missing content.

Updating a source record is reflected in a newly generated PDF, while an older export remains unchanged.

Use this checklist during testing. Checking an item here tracks your review only; it does not test the actual application.

### 8.2 CV-specific acceptance scenarios

| Scenario                          | Expected result                                                                     |
| --------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Create a CV for Data Analyst      | A saved configuration exists with the specified role and template.                  |
| Receive content suggestions       | Suggested records have a visible reason or relevant category.                       |
| Reject a suggestion               | The entry is excluded from that CV but remains in the career database.              |
| Add a project manually            | The selected project appears in the preview and export.                             |
| Duplicate a CV                    | The new version inherits the initial configuration without changing the original.   |
| Change the summary for one CV     | Other CV versions and the main profile summary remain unchanged.                    |
| Select a long project description | The preview identifies overflow or the export handles pagination correctly.         |
| Generate a new PDF                | The downloaded file reflects the latest saved data and selected layout.             |
| Delete a record used by a CV      | The administrator is warned and affected selections are handled safely.             |
| Publish a CV                      | Only the explicitly selected CV becomes available through its intended public link. |

### 8.3 Portfolio acceptance scenarios

* The homepage displays the current published profile headline and featured projects.

* A project with no demo link does not display a broken or empty demo button.

* A project with incomplete optional details still renders correctly.

* Unpublishing a project removes it from public listings and its public detail page.

* The portfolio remains usable on mobile without horizontal scrolling for ordinary content.

* Public contact details follow the owner's visibility settings.

## 9. Non-functional requirements

These requirements describe the quality and operational behavior expected from the product.

| Category        | Requirement                                                                                                            |
| --------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Security        | Private management pages require authentication and server-side authorization.                                         |
| Privacy         | Only explicitly published information may appear in public responses.                                                  |
| Usability       | Forms have clear labels, validation feedback and save/cancel actions.                                                  |
| Responsiveness  | Dashboard, portfolio and CV workflows work on desktop and mobile.                                                      |
| Accessibility   | Forms have accessible labels, keyboard navigation and sufficient contrast.                                             |
| Reliability     | Successful saves persist when the user returns to the record.                                                          |
| Data integrity  | CV selections reference valid career records; deleted records are handled safely.                                      |
| Performance     | Ordinary portfolio pages and dashboard views should load without unnecessary delays under normal personal-use traffic. |
| PDF quality     | Exported files have selectable text, consistent headings, correct ordering and readable pagination.                    |
| Maintainability | Career data, portfolio presentation and CV presentation remain logically separate.                                     |
| Portability     | The administrator can export a backup of their career data in a documented format.                                     |
| Compatibility   | Public pages and PDF previews work in current mainstream desktop and mobile browsers.                                  |

### ATS compatibility and validation

The product should aim for a CV that is easy for applicant tracking systems to parse, not claim that it is guaranteed to pass every ATS.

The MVP should prioritize:

* Standard headings such as Experience, Education, Skills and Projects.

* A logical text-reading order.

* Selectable text rather than a flattened image.

* Consistent dates, role titles and organization names.

* Meaningful hyperlinks and no essential information conveyed only by icons or color.

* PDF text extraction tests to verify that the document's content remains intact.

## 10. MVP release checklist

The MVP is ready for release when the following end-to-end workflow succeeds:

### Release acceptance

0 of 8 release criteria checked

Administrator can log in, manage the profile and securely log out.

Career records can be created, edited, deleted and organized.

Published portfolio pages reflect saved public information.

Private records remain hidden from public visitors.

At least two CV templates are available and previewable.

Multiple saved CV configurations support automatic suggestions and manual selection.

PDF export works, and the resulting document passes text and layout checks.

The online portfolio and its public URLs work on desktop and mobile.

The release is ready only after each item has been verified against the actual application.

## 11. Final product definition

## MyCareerSpace — MVP summary

One owner · One career database · Three outputs

A personal, web-based portfolio and ATS CV management application that lets an administrator maintain professional information through structured forms, selectively publish it to a public portfolio, and generate multiple role-specific CV PDFs from saved configurations.

Private side: Profile and career CRUD, publication controls, CV configuration management, templates and PDF export.

Public side: A hybrid minimalist portfolio with About, Projects, Experience and Contact pages.

CV workflow: Role-based suggestions, manual content selection, section ordering, template preview, validation and PDF download.

Future direction: AI-assisted writing and job-description matching, introduced only after the core workflow is reliable.

The next logical planning artifact is a page-by-page wireframe specification. It should define the dashboard layout, each form's fields, the portfolio page sections, the CV selection screen and the PDF preview, so the user experience is settled before implementation begins.
